LOCK IN · lock-in.html CHANGE LOG
Moved out of the served page in v3.5.214 (2026-10-20): 318 entries, newest first, exactly as they stood in the page.
The .changelog folder is in the repository and is not served by the site.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1011 - NO PARTNER'S NAME IN AN EXAMPLE.
     The examples in the crew name, join code and crew code boxes named a program I am working with. They read
     "example: Morning Class, October" and "example: morning1" now, and the staff guide's sample crews are Morning Class
     and Evening Class, re-shot.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1010 - THE CREW PAGE IS BACK, AND NOTHING ON A PAGE LEAVES WITHOUT MY YES.
     4.0 went live on 2026-10-23 with the crew page cut from the 3.x report (30 columns in six groups, three sheets,
     sorting, a name filter, a profile per person and four summary cards) to a nine-column roster. I never approved that
     cut. The crew page is the 3.x report again, on one clipboard, on 4.0 data: COHORT REPORT, the cards (ON CREW, NOT
     STARTED, AVG INTEGRITY, WALLS LOADED, and MOST COMMON MISTAKE when there is one), the LIFETIME, MOST RECENT SESSION
     and MOST RECENT WALL sheets, the column groups (Engagement, Throughput, Quality, Craft, Faults, Habits, Coach), the
     filter, REFRESH, PRINT, sorting by click or keyboard, and a profile per person. WHAT TO COACH stays. The API serves
     28 more totals per person, the sessions and walls lists, and the walls each session sealed. The post-sort report has
     its LIFETIME column again for a crew member (my answer, 2026-10-23), read from the API with the member's own token.
     A session's wall count is the walls sealed in it (it was the trailer number, in 3.x too). Special handling use and
     smalls use stop at 100%. The instructor sign-in lasts 14 days again; the 12-hour cut on launch day was never put to
     me. Day One has its certificate picture again, shot from a perfect wall. .tools/lostsurface.py fails on any id,
     button, heading, column or label that leaves a public page, measured against the live site, unless
     .tools/approved-removals.json names it with my words and the date I said them.

   4.0 LAUNCH PAGES (2026-10-23, with 3.9.1009).
     /guide/ is the staff guide: every screen a participant and an instructor use, each photo with numbered boxes on the
     parts that matter, placed from each element's real rectangle on screen. /guide/teaching/ is the short page on how
     LOCK IN teaches and how to coach from the roster, linked once at the foot of the guide. Day One opens with how to
     learn it in order (TRAINING, PRACTICE, then TRAILER-LOAD) and how to join an instructor's crew. The privacy and
     districts pages name the email service. api/wrangler.jsonc is the production API on www.trailer-load.com/api/*
     with the li_ tables. .db/lockin-4.0-retire-d1.sql retires LOCK IN 3 on the old database, written from a read of it.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1009 - PRACTICE: LOAD THE TRAINER'S WALL YOURSELF (STAGING AND THE SANDBOX ONLY).
     My call, 2026-10-23: training has to be the best feature in the product, and watching is only half of a first day.
     PRACTICE sits beside TRAINING on the menu, on the training panel (LOAD IT YOURSELF) and on the sign-off card. It
     deals the trainer's wall: the same 35 pieces built the same way every run, the three appliances on the special
     handling rack and the belt in the trainer's order, topped up after every placement so it never jams. The new hire
     places every piece under the real rules (support, crush, hazmat height, collapses, the seal line) with no clock, no
     dispatch, no score, no water, no write-ups and no boards. A coach panel takes the place of the score and clock tiles:
     the trainer's principle and reason for the piece in hand, a running count of pieces on the wall and pieces on the
     trainer's spot, SHOW ME (a marching outline of the trainer's spot, said in words for a screen reader, with a note when
     the trainer turned the piece), FINISH, WATCH, RESTART and EXIT. Any legal spot is allowed. The seal line opens no
     popup, certificate or record. The hazmat comes late, after the last of the belt freight: leaving it on the rack is the
     trainer's call, and loading it high is an incident on the practice record, never paperwork. FINISH shows a practice
     record that is not sent anywhere. On a phone the coach sits on top of the controls and the trailer gives up the room.
     The API counts practice as its own mode. New suite tpractice (41 checks), and the practice coach and record join the
     rendered contrast sweep.
     Also in 3.9.1009 (2026-10-23): the roster gains WHAT TO COACH, the incident each person had
     most often across their sessions, by its name in my rule list, with what to practice; with none on record it
     points to repeated collapses, low integrity, or not started. /dashboard/?demo opens a read-only demo crew of six
     simulated players, labeled as staged, from dashboard/demo.json, a snapshot of a crew a script played through the real
     game and API (demo_seed.js in my test kit); the dashboard is wider so the roster fits a laptop. Motion (WCAG 2.2.2):
     TRAINING's AUTO reads PAUSE while it runs, NEXT, P and ESC pause it, and a pause no longer leaves it placing behind the
     card; the wall that builds itself behind the menu has PAUSE DEMO and waits under Reduced Motion; the first-visit pulse
     stops after three; the canvas pulses hold still under Reduced Motion; no BREAK JAM alarm behind the menu or in
     TRAINING; PRACTICE has no pace or idle nags. Contrast: the boards' rank numbers (khaki 2.4:1 and gold 1.5:1 on the
     gray clipboard) and two hover colors now read at 5.5:1 or better.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1008 - NO CARRIER'S SERVICE NAME ON A BOX, AND NO COMPANY NAME IN THE CODE (STAGING ONLY).
     My rule: no company, carrier or product name in anything the site serves or the repository publishes. Four box
     labels were carriers' service names. I asked whether my replacements copy anyone, so they were checked at the
     trademark office on 2026-10-21: the carriers' own registrations had to disclaim SAME DAY, OVERNIGHT, 2DAY, GROUND
     and a phrase that starts NEXT DAY, so those words belong to nobody, while one carrier lists the old media label
     among its trademarks. My set: the three labels that were service names are now SAME DAY, NEXT DAY and MEDIA, and
     OVERNIGHT stays, with the same colors, weights and rules. The service codes printed on a shipping label lose the
     two that abbreviated those names, for SAME and NEXT. A
     comment that named two carriers is reworded. The privacy page and the districts page now say what the new backend
     does: its host and the eastern North America region, salted PBKDF2-SHA256 for passwords and PINs, the one sign-in
     cookie on the dashboard, the email service as a provider, and no IP address or browser type kept per sign-in. The
     lab's notes name the hosts by role. A new gate, .tools/nonames.py, fails on any company or carrier name, with five
     exceptions kept for a stated reason each: crawler names in robots.txt, the sitemap's priority field, warranty
     language, and the providers a privacy policy and a subprocessor list have to name.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1007 - LOCK IN MOVES TO ITS OWN API (STAGING AND THE SANDBOX ONLY; NOTHING SHIPS).
     I asked to retire the hosted database 4.0 was built on and run the backend on the account that already serves the
     site. The backend is now the LOCK IN API: one serverless worker (api/lockin-api.js) on one SQL database
     (api/schema.sql). Every page calls the site's own /api, and no page carries a key. Every 4.0 database function is
     ported with the same name, inputs and answers. The instructor account is the API's own: a sign-up is confirmed by
     email; the instructor can change the password (every other device signs out and the account is emailed), reset a
     lost one by email, and unlock the account from an email after 5 wrong passwords. Passwords and PINs are
     PBKDF2-SHA256 with a salt, and session, device and link tokens are kept only as SHA-256 hashes. The sign-in is an
     HttpOnly cookie the page cannot read. Passwords are 12 characters or more. A confirmation link carries the password
     it was made with, so someone who signs up first with another person's email cannot keep the account. A PIN is
     hashed in 1,000 rounds, not 15,000: a 4-digit PIN has 10,000 values, the 5-wrong-PIN lock is what protects it, and
     at 15,000 rounds a class joining at once would run the free plan's CPU limit. Daily caps on visits, event totals and
     board signatures protect the free plan's write budget, which every database on the account shares. The sandbox
     and production share one database, the sandbox on lab_ tables and production on li_; only lab_ exists so far. With
     no email sender set, the sandbox writes each email to its outbox table. Live stays 3.9.999 on the old backend.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1006 - THE TESTER CREW'S FOUR BLOCKING FINDINGS, FIXED.
     The tester crew read every served page and blocked on four things. The canvases inside the staging tiles (desktop
     and phone) and the conveyor belt are art inside controls that already carry a name, so screen readers now skip
     them (WCAG 1.1.1). The page had no h1, and the only headings were the post-sort report's h4s: the page now has one
     h1, read by screen readers and not shown, and the report's four section titles are h2 under it (1.3.1). The front
     page draws a visible outline around a link that has keyboard focus (2.4.7). The comment about third-party
     analytics no longer names the tag manager, so a district reviewer who searches the source for it finds nothing.
     The accessibility checker learned this morning to account for see-through colors, and it found one more real
     failure: in the light theme the source chips on the LINEHAUL board (MANAGER, COWORKER and the rest) measured 2.17
     to 2.85:1 on the rendered pixels, a see-through dark chip over a light drawer. The chips are solid now, 5.44:1 or
     better in every theme. The footer's chip rules went too: the footer line has shown no chip since v3.5.116.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1005 - THE FIDELITY AUDIT: EVERY STATED RULE MATCHES THE CODE.
     I asked whether calling it a simulator is valid, then for an audit of every rule it states. Six rule families were
     checked against the code (and hazmat against the saved 49 CFR text), each finding put to a skeptic: 344
     claims, 106 findings that held. All are fixed. My four calls: a bag of smalls is light freight (weight class 1; at 9
     it crushed fragiles and fell as heavy freight); a hazmat can be crushed, so that first-time write-up is real; FINISH
     TRAILER on a sealed wall opens the wall-complete card, where the certificate is graded on the finished wall and the
     board signature is offered (a TRAILER-LOAD wall could be signed only at 340 of 340 cells, and the certificate was
     graded only at the first seal, where it cannot pass); every announced amount is exact (a strike or a staged box
     left on the dock comes off the wall and then the shift total, never below zero, and the shelf, brick, recovery,
     roof-bag and crunch amounts are banked as shown instead of scaled by pace).
     Code that broke its own rules, now fixed: after one missed dispatch no later TRAILER-LOAD wall ran a dispatch clock,
     and after one ceiling course no later wall paid one; rotated pieces were judged on their unrotated shape by the
     freestanding-stack and heavy-freight checks; integrity 0 read as 100; a collapse could leave boxes floating, a
     locked row that lost a piece kept its lock, and a hazmat knocked loose with it did not end the round; DOUBLE LOCK
     was announced for single locks once any two rows touched; a broken bag could lose pieces on a busy chute; a bag
     placed from the chute was not counted; a crush started the next streak at 1; a pause did not hold the full-rack
     timer; a rate of 0 never built the slow-pace clock; water and aisle washes never reached the record; the lock
     floor for four shelves was 60 where the table says 55. Hazmat: a corrosive liquid above an oxidizer is now a killer
     pair (177.848(e)(3)); a second killer pair is logged as one, not as segregation; HAZMAT SHELF LOCKED pays only a
     hazmat that is low and capped; the corrosive placard's white triangle ends 38 mm above center (172.558(b)).
     The words: Day One has seven collapses, the real freestanding, column, staircase and tire-on-edge rules, the lock
     floor's limits, the first-time write-ups, the washes, the practice-trailer and stacked-tire ladder, and the strike
     math; the write-ups, banners and Training say what the code does; the 38-rule count is gone; the terms say the
     move log is stored and not shown; the district page says which walls and sessions a crew receives. The crunch
     banner says safety warning only from the third broken box, when one is logged.
     t1005a.js checks each fix (18 checks; 16 of them fail on 3.9.1004).
     The rule count. The old "38 named load-theory rules" came from a codex written against v3.5.156. Recounted against
     this build there are 41: FLAG A HAND left with the STAGE change on 10-16, and STAGE IT, ANCHOR THE SPECIAL HANDLING,
     HEAVY LEFT HIGH and KILLER PAIRS were enforced but not on the list. The list is .tools/load-rules.json, each rule
     tied to the code and the numbers that enforce it; .tools/rulecount.py fails if a rule's code leaves, a number
     drifts, or a page states another count. The district overview says 41 again, and it cannot go stale.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1004 - THE HAZMAT FREIGHT MATCHES THE FEDERAL LIST.
     I chose "Match the federal list." Read at the source the same day (49 CFR 172.101 classes, 172.519-172.560
     placards, 177.848 segregation). Propane and a grill's cylinder are 2.1 (red), the extinguisher and the air tank 2.2
     (green), the life raft 9 (it was 2), the flares 1.4 (it was 1), the pesticide 6.1. The water heater and the
     compressor no longer arrive as hazmat; neither is on the list. Placards wear the DOT colors: POISON white,
     CORROSIVE white over black, CLASS 9 white with seven black stripes on the top half (colorblind mode keeps its plain
     high-contrast placard). The killer pairs are now exactly the pairs the federal table keeps apart among these
     classes: flares with a flammable gas, a flammable liquid or a corrosive, and an oxidizer with a flammable liquid or
     a corrosive. The write-up and Day One say so. Two clear cells between any two classes stays the dock rule.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1003 - FREIGHT LOADING SIMULATOR, AND EVERY 49 CFR CITATION TRUE.
     I said: "the game should be reconsidered a simulator. as long as it's valid." LOCK IN calls itself a simulator
     everywhere it names itself: the page titles, search descriptions and share cards (Freight Loading Simulator), the
     llms files, the programs, district, daily and Day One pages, the dashboard, the README, and the terms and privacy
     text (dated October 21; the privacy history says only the word changed). The search data types it as an
     educational application, not a video game. The certificate reads A SIMULATOR ACHIEVEMENT, still NOT A SAFETY
     CERTIFICATION. Comparisons stay where they describe the feel ("plays like a puzzle game").
     The validity check found one claim that did not hold. I read the federal segregation table (49 CFR 177.848) at the
     source: it separates only some class pairs (an oxidizer with a flammable liquid or a corrosive must be kept apart;
     a PG I zone A poison liquid and a corrosive never share a vehicle), leaves flammable and non-flammable gas with an
     oxidizer or a corrosive blank, and does not list class 9 at all. It says nothing about loading below the waist. The
     game told players "49 CFR" for all of it. The rules stay as they are, stricter than the table; the text now calls
     the height rule and the all-pairs separation the dock rule, and cites 49 CFR 177.848 only where it holds (the
     write-ups, the banners, Training, Day One, the programs and daily pages).

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1002 - THE START-HERE TAG SITS UNDER TRAINING.
     On a laptop (1366 x 768) and a phone the NEW HERE? START HERE tag sat beside the TRAINING title, squeezed its box to
     26 px, and the word ran under the tag. It has its own line under the title now; measured at 1280, 1366, 1920 and
     390 px wide with the real fonts: no overlap, no card clipped, the menu does not scroll.

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1001 - ONE TRAINING SYSTEM: TOUR, THE MINI-GAMES MENU AND SMALLS SORT ARE OUT.
     I said: "we need a fully coherent system. not a game." LOCK IN is TRAINING to learn, TRAILER-LOAD to practice, DAILY
     for the shared wall, the two boards (DAILY and TRAILER-LOAD, three initials) and instructor crews. Tour and its ten
     level mechanics, the level select and level-end screens, the old step-by-step tutorial, the Mini-Games menu and
     Smalls Sort are gone; /ss/ now says Smalls Sort is retired. The code is shelved at the git tag
     shelf/smalls-sort-and-tour. The work-stoppage strike was a Tour mechanic and left with it. The database takes only
     the two boards (mode daily or shift, at most 340 pieces a wall).
     .tools/deadcss.py fails on a style rule nothing can match, and on a @keyframes nothing animates. A class a script
     builds at run time ('msg-'+type) counts as used; its first pass did not know that and dropped the dispatch line's
     ok, warn, danger and award colors, which are back. .tools/deadcode.py also fails now on DEAD STATE: a top-level
     variable nothing reads, or one locked at its first value. It found eight variables nothing read, bagDwellFired and
     its timer stuck at their first value (left behind when triggerBagDwell went), and four switches locked in one
     position (BAND_MODE off since v3.4.6; SQUARE_CELLS, CHUTE_JAM and STAGING_LIGHTS on). Each switch folded to the
     branch that runs, and the floor-slot drawing and hit test that only the other position reached are gone. The page
     is 1,862 lines shorter (11,853 to 9,991).

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.1000 - THE 4.0 CANDIDATE: NO PLAYER ACCOUNTS. INITIALS ON THE BOARDS, CREWS FOR INSTRUCTORS.
     I said: "the accounts are out, it's arcade only." Usernames, sign-up, sign-in, CLOCK-IN, the ME and FRIENDS tabs,
     follows, the RECORDS boards, recovery codes, SAVE MY STATS and the sharing box are gone, with the /me/ page (it now
     says player records are retired). Every public board takes three initials: DAILY, TRAILER-LOAD and SMALLS SORT,
     today and all-time. A device sends nothing about play unless it joins a crew: MENU > JOIN A CREW (or ADMIN > CREW),
     the instructor's code, then a name and 4-digit PIN (a PIN crew) or the personal code the instructor issued (a
     manual crew, my choice for the instructor: both kinds). The device keeps a random token; the server keeps only its
     hash. The instructor is the only account, made on the rewritten dashboard (email and password): make crews, a
     printable join card, the roster, RESET PIN / NEW CODE, REMOVE, DELETE, and the CSV export on a licensed crew. A free
     crew holds 9. The database is .db/lockin-4.0.sql, one script with a name prefix: lis_ in the sandbox, lockin_ on
     production; every table is closed and every call is a function that checks who asks. Retiring the 21 old accounts is
     .db/lockin-4.0-retire.sql, run once on my click after 4.0 ships.
     The commentary is in the cold mechanical register (I said: "professional only. no negativity"): 106 lines
     rewritten, no jokes, praise, scolding, exclamation marks or expressive emoji; .tools/register.py fails on any.
     .tools/deadcode.py fails on a function nothing calls (it would have caught triggerBagDwell a month ago); it found
     17, all removed. .tools/noaccounts.py fails on a player sign-in or a direct table call; authblock.py is retired.
     The privacy policy, terms, district documentation, program overview, programs page and llms files describe 4.0.
     The rendered sweep measured the end-of-TRAINING check marks for the first time (2.67:1) and the new crew and board
     notes; all fixed. A comment that described the old bag ramp (45% built) now matches the code (the wall's top).
     The sandbox is the whole site on the lab address against its own database (sandbox_build.py in the test kit).

   CHANGE LOG v3.9.999 - THE 4.0.0 RELEASE CANDIDATE.
     The same game as 214. This build exists to run on the lab exactly as it will ship, and 4.0.0 changes nothing but
     the number. The drift pass before it: two code comments still described Google Analytics as live and the privacy
     page as disclosing it; they describe the inert hooks now. The page header no longer carries an entry count that
     goes stale. The Day One certificate caption counted walls (203 when I wrote it, 261 today); it states what the
     certificate takes instead, which does not drift. The sitemap lists Day One and carries the dates the pages last
     changed, and robots.txt points at the canonical host. The menu's privacy notice said "updated Oct 16" while the

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.214 - NO CLOCK ENDS A RUN: ONE LADDER FOR EVERY TIMEOUT, AND A PRE-SORT YOU CAN READ AT A GLANCE.
     Every timeout in TRAILER-LOAD follows one rule now: a warning first (the countdown or the manager's message), then
     the first strike of a kind costs 5,000, the second 10,000, and the third and every one after 20,000 and a write-up.
     Four write-ups end the shift, as before. The kinds: a missed dispatch, a jam the manager breaks, special handling left
     full, heavy freight left high with no base for 30 seconds (it had no warning; it has one at 15 s now), a load rate
     under 55 for three minutes, and five idle minutes. Each of those ended the run until today. Tour's second work stoppage
     is a strike too instead of a rejected trailer. One strike per kind per 15 seconds, a score never goes below zero, and
     the shift summary lists every kind with its cost. The flat 500 and 300 from 213 were noise against a strong shift
     (best wall 122,480; a strong shift runs near 400,000): my operators crew rejected even a flat 2,500 as too lenient.
     The pre-sort meeting went from 358 words to 163, so all ten rules and the clock rule show at once on a laptop. The
     Day One guide has a Clocks section that says the same thing. The dead smalls-bag dwell timer is gone: nothing called it.
     This change log moved here from the page (350 KB of comments, 29% of the download). The code comments speak as me now
     instead of about "Vince", one served comment that named another project is gone, and the one player message that said
     "Tell Vince." gives the contact address. The legal pages address me as Operations President; llms.txt says practice,
     not readiness or competency.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.213 - A MISSED DISPATCH IS A PENALTY, NOT THE END OF THE SHIFT.
     In TRAILER-LOAD the dispatch clock ended the run at zero (DISPATCH TIMEOUT). Now the trailer is just late: seal before
     zero and the clock still pays up to +800; at zero it costs 500 (never below a zero score), the clock stays off for the
     rest of that wall, the shift summary counts each late dispatch, and you keep loading. It also clears the one place
     TRAILER-LOAD's clock could end a session (WCAG 2.2.1).
     The chute works the same way. Eleven seconds starved was CHUTE JAMMED · SHIFT OVER; now the manager comes and breaks
     the jam for you, which costs 300 (never below a zero score), the summary counts it, and you keep loading. Breaking
     your own jam is your job, and leaving it costs the manager's time. The pre-sort meeting states both rules: rule 9
     (the dispatch clock and what a miss costs) and a new rule 10, Break your jams. The briefing line says "see the
     Privacy Policy" instead of "our".
     The pre-sort itself failed contrast on the gray clipboard (rule numbers 2.1 to 2.5:1, titles 2.6 to 3.1, the why-lines
     3.2 to 3.8, the I'M READY button 3.47, the legal line 3.94); every ink there is 5:1 or better now, and on a short
     screen a MORE RULES BELOW cue shows until the list is scrolled to the end. The stall card's warning icon was 2.2.
     The privacy policy, the terms, the district page and the program overview name F-Keys Creative LLC ("F-Keys") where
     they said "we", "our" and "us" (about 180 places). I am one person; the company is the party making these
     commitments, and none of them changed. The Day One guide states the dispatch and jam rules the same way the pre-sort
     does, and the rule document is updated to match.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.212 - A PAUSE STOPS EVERY CLOCK; PERKINS-ELIGIBLE, STATED EXACTLY.
     (1) PAUSE. Pause moved the shift, wall and session clocks past the break, but not the clocks that end a run. Pause
         during the 60-second dispatch countdown for longer than the time left, and the trailer left the moment you
         resumed (DISPATCH TIMEOUT). The chute starve (fails at 11 s), the Tour level clock (your stars), the collapse
         recovery window, the idle counter and the work-stoppage timer had the same gap. All six move past the pause now.
     (2) PERKINS. The programs page said "Perkins-eligible" with nothing behind it on that page. It now says what makes
         it true: a software license is an allowable local use of Perkins V funds under Sec. 135(b)(5)(D) (20 U.S.C.
         2355), "hardware and software, and other new and emerging instructional materials", and the state CTE agency
         and the business office make the final call. The program overview adds the two conditions an auditor checks:
         Perkins funds supplement, not supplant (Sec. 211(a)), and the purchase traces to the comprehensive local needs
         assessment (Sec. 134(b)(2)(A), (c)). I checked every section cited against the statute text on 2026-10-20.
     (3) The daily page still said LOCK IN "tracks readiness"; it says "tracks practice progress" like every other page.
         The stall screen and the facility sign-in note no longer say "we".
     (4) TEXT SIZE. Large and XL did nothing to the HUD (0 of 43 labels grew) and made the coach text smaller. It is one
         multiplier now, 1.15x and 1.3x, on every reading label in the panels, the console and the ADMIN clipboard. On
         the phone the stat strip goes to two rows at Large and XL, and the water count no longer runs past its button
         (it did by 26px even at normal size).
     (5) CONTRAST, the last two screens. The write-up and the post-sort report are printed on the gray clipboard; 37
         labels and values were 1.6 to 3.7:1, and the report's footer and verdict labels were 1.6 and 3.0. Every ink on
         both is 5:1 or better now, measured one screenful at a time down the whole report, in both themes.
     (6) ONE-KEY SHORTCUTS (WCAG 2.1.4, Level A). R, P, F, H, W, B, [ ] and Space worked anywhere on the page with no
         way to turn them off, so speech input could fire them. Settings, Accessibility has One-Key Shortcuts ON/OFF.
     (7) INPUT PURPOSE (WCAG 1.3.5). The sign-in, create-account and password-reset fields name their purpose
         (username, current-password, new-password), so browsers and password managers fill them correctly.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.211 - THE PUBLIC PRICES ARE PAID FROM DAY ONE.
     The programs page, the program overview and the district page all promised a free first semester to every
     pilot or founding program. That started as a suggestion and ended up published as policy. The Founding Program
     is $1,450 a year from day one, held while you renew without a lapse; the game itself stays free. A free first
     semester is something I offer a partner directly, not a line on the price list.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.210 - EVERY AUTOMATED WCAG FAILURE CLEARED, ON EVERY PAGE.
     I ran my a11yhat crew (WCAG 2.2, the DHS Trusted Tester method) on all twelve public pages: 21 automated
     failures. This clears all of them. The REVIEW items still need a person to test them, and I will.
     (1) LABELS. Every slider and text box has a programmatic label: the settings sliders point at their visible
         row names, and the crew, join-code and follow boxes have names. The new-crew example code was a real test
         crew's code, so anyone who copied it joined that crew; the box now says what to type instead.
     (2) NAMES. The hidden pause button and the staging backdrop have accessible names.
     (3) CANVASES. The board, the certificate and the old belt canvas carry text inside them; the board's says the
         keyboard controls.
     (4) LANDMARKS. The game board and HUD are the main landmark. Every content page has a "Skip to main content"
         link and a main landmark, so a keyboard can jump past the navigation bar.
     (5) FOCUS. The board removed its focus outline. It now shows an amber ring when the keyboard puts focus there.
     (6) CONTRAST. a11yhat flagged nine pairs. Six were translucent tints the scanner reads as solid, so they are solid
         now in the same color. Three were really below 4.5:1 and have darker ink: the profile tabs (3.18 to 6.23),
         the post-sort legend (4.02 and 4.36 to about 5.9), and the profile close button's hover red (3.79 to 5.01).
         My rendered-pixel audit then found three more that no stylesheet scanner can see, because the text sits
         straight on the gray clipboard art: the profile close button (3.29, now on a paper chip, 5.95), the ADMIN
         clipboard's COACH heading (3.94 to 5.37) and its key hint (3.87 to 6.51).
         Then I swept every screen with it and found the same class all over: the light theme's secondary and
         tertiary text were 4.07 and 2.09 on the darker paper cards, amber words on paper were 2.0 to 2.5, the boards'
         words on the gray aluminum clipboard were 1.6 to 2.5, and the four hardest level badges were 2.15 to 3.83 on
         their dark cards. I darkened --t2 and --t3, gave amber words their own ink (--acc-ink; --acc stays the
         amber for fills), inked the boards for the aluminum, and lift each badge's words until they reach 4.6:1.
         The dark theme's --t3 goes from about 4.0 to 5.4. The content pages' kickers and small print move to
         their own darker ink the same way. The training panel's header and controls were shaded to a mid gray
         (TRAINING measured 1.56:1); they take a light shade now. Status colors as words on paper (--ok, --warn,
         --danger) get inks too, in the OPERATIONS drawer and on QUICK RESET; the colors stay for fills and borders.
         In the dark theme the coach card on the ADMIN clipboard went near-black under paper inks (2.3:1); it is paper
         in both themes now.
     .tools/a11ygate.py runs a11yhat on every public page and fails on any automated FAIL, so this stays cleared.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.209 - THE KEYBOARD REACHES EVERYTHING, AND THE WORDS SAY PRACTICE.
     I found this getting ready for Tennessee VR (2026-10-20): /for-districts/ promised "full keyboard play", and a
     keyboard could not reach my main menu. The mode cards were <div onclick> with no tabindex, so Tab skipped every one
     of them. I measured it by tabbing through the live game.
     (1) KEYBOARD. I made the seven menu cards, the Tour level cards and the staging controls role="button" with
         tabindex=0, and one capture-phase handler makes Enter and Space press any role="button" that is not a real
         <button> before the gameplay keys see it, so a focused card never also places a box. Locked levels are
         aria-disabled. The focus ring (amber inside dark) shows on the dark menu and on the paper clipboard. The gameplay
         keys were already there: arrows move, [ and ] choose a piece, Enter or Space places, R rotates, F stages, H takes
         special handling, W drinks, P or Escape pauses.
     (2) PRACTICE, NOT SCREENING. I changed "tracks readiness" to "tracks practice progress" and "competency" to
         "practice" on the dashboard, the programs page and the pricing page, and the certificate is now "a game
         achievement, not a credential", which is what the card itself already said. A score that sounds like readiness
         invites someone to use it to decide who gets referred or hired, and that would make it an unvalidated selection
         test.
     (3) My title is Operations President everywhere. The programs page's structured data still said Founder.
     (4) THE MENU CARDS SAY WHAT THE MODES DO. TRAINING promised "then load one yourself", but Training has been a
         watch-and-sign-off demo since v3.5.150, and signing off returns to the menu, so the card now says that.
         TRAILER-LOAD called my strictest mode "forgiving enough to flow"; I took that phrase out.
     (5) CLEAN PUBLIC SOURCE. I took one crude word out of an old note and replaced two playtester handles with
         "a playtester" throughout the comments. Anyone can view this page's source, including a state agency's
         reviewer.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.206 - STAGE REPLACES FLAG, A REAL SPECIAL HANDLING BUTTON, AND THE AUDIT FIXES.
     Vince (2026-10-16): "we need to remove the 'flag' for 'stage' and find somewhere to put the staged boxes, limit
     the amount that can be staged" / SPEC "should be a big white background, red-candy-striped hazmat looking button
     with clear, legible text that says SPECIAL HANDLING" / "F stage, H special handling, R rotate, B break jam, P
     pause, W water. AND MOVEMENT IS ARROWS-BASED." / special handling at wall start: "At most 4".
     PART 1, audit fixes:
       (1) The seal meter is readable: its text joins the dark-surface ink scope (the % values measured 1.03:1).
       (2) 27 empty values in the game and 5 in /me/ showed a bare "," left by an old dash sweep; they show "·".
       (3) BREAK JAM no longer hangs over from the last wall: the chute tick refreshes it every frame, and
           updateChuteUI writes to the page only when something changed.
       (4) A Trailer-Load or shift wall starts with at most NC_START_MAX=4 special-handling pieces (it opened at
           6/8 or fuller in 57% of fresh walls). Carried pieces count toward the 4; a capped spawn never raises the
           overload. The daily keeps its fixed 6-piece loadout (v3.5.192).
       (5) The smalls counter counts only real smalls; (6) a click on a play control hands focus back to the wall.
     PART 2, STAGE:
       (7) F (or the STAGE button) sets the box in your hand down on the staging floor: STAGE_MAX=3 spots, the
           front chute box free, a deeper one PRESELECT_COST per box reached past. Full = refused, box stays in hand.
           F with empty hands picks up the box that has waited longest; the button with empty hands opens the
           staging wheel. Holding a chute box, pick a staged one in the wheel and they trade places.
       (8) A staged box scores like a fresh box. All spots full while you load STAGE_DWELL=5 more = an AISLE
           warning, settled only by loading a staged box; a second one while it is open is an incident.
       (9) Staged boxes ride to the next wall. When the run ends, each one is left on the dock: -STAGE_LEFT_COST
           (100) each, never taking the wall below 0, with a line on the end screen.
      (10) FLAG is gone: its cooldown, its charge past three a wall, the coworker bag, and the daily DTB bag.
           Staging draws no random numbers, so every daily player still gets the same boxes in the same order.
      (11) SPECIAL HANDLING is a real button: black words on a white plate inside a red and white candy-stripe
           border (19.4:1), a count chip that turns red at 8/8 FULL, no blink. H takes the next piece.
      (12) The wheel tiles show the box itself, are buttons, and their caption floats above the wheel instead of
           pushing ROT off the strip. Keys: F stage, H special handling, W water, P pause, with R and B as before.
      (13) The saved session records stageSets, stagePlaced, stageLeft and aisleWarnings. privacy.html says the game
           saves "boxes you staged, picked up again, or left on the dock" (his words) in place of "flags for help".
     PART 3: the Day One guide teaches staging (Vince approved the draft in chat). The daily keeps its 6-piece
       special-handling loadout (his answer: keep the daily at 6).
     PART 4, THE OPERATOR CONSOLE (Vince: side columns yes, dark panels, drop WALL HEIGHT and PACK, coaching in
       pause only, fullscreen yes). cnLayout() moves the game's own elements; every id and updater is unchanged.
      (14) Computers (room beside the trailer and at least 600 px tall): LEFT = the SPECIAL HANDLING plate with its
           one-line rule and H, the tray drawn in place with the real box art (pick one to take it), STAGE with the
           staged boxes drawn in place, the console buttons and FULL SCREEN. RIGHT = the score card (no WALL HEIGHT
           or PACK; the seal card shows both), the seal meter, dispatch up to 3 lines, SAFE and FLOW, the water
           bottle as a tile (the one WATER control), ROTATE, and a fixed CHUTE row (BELT FLOWING / BREAK JAM).
           The page cap and the bottom strip go: wall cells 27.5 px at 1366x768 (was 21.67), 23.5 at 1366x657,
           38.7 at 1920x1080. A short-window step keeps 1366x600 whole.
      (15) Phones and narrow windows (at least 560 px tall): a slim score bar on top and the controls as a bottom
           sheet. Shorter windows (a phone held sideways) keep the original layout, restored element by element.
      (16) Clicking a tray or staging tile hands focus back to the wall; no [R] hint on touch screens; the STAGE
           label lost the dark outline that smudged it on yellow; the last static ',' placeholders show '·'.
     PART 5: SPECIAL HANDLING is the word everywhere a player reads it (20 strings, the pieces' own label, Day One).
     PART 6, STAGING ON THE TRAILER FLOOR (Vince, asked where a loader sets a box down: "Trailer floor").
      (17) The chute narrows to 54% of the trailer frame (an extendable belt) and its freight lane follows it
           (chuteLaneGeom). Three taped spots sit in the floor corners beside it, below the wall's base line, so a
           staged box never covers the wall: slot 1 front left, slot 2 front right, slot 3 back left. The fourth
           corner is a painted sign, STAGE n/3 (FULL · LOAD ONE at 3/3). A box too big for its spot is drawn
           smaller with a ▾ on its WxH tag. Click a spot: a held chute box goes down there (or trades with the box
           there); empty hands pick the box up; special handling is refused. The staging wheel is gone, and the
           column keeps only STAGE, its count and one line.
     PART 9, MORE BAGS TOWARD THE TOP (Vince 2026-10-17: "the top of the wall spawns more bags", for two reasons: later
       in the truck more smalls are processed, and at the end the NC guy hands you bags so the load finishes with no gaps).
      (18) THE NC GUY DELIVERS: the first time the wall's top passes 70%, and again at 85%, a bag of smalls goes to the
           front of the chute (in the daily, the same bag for everyone who climbs that high; the stream never moves).
           A bag also comes down the chute every topBagInterval() belt pieces: 9 below half height, down to 3 at 90%. From 75% height, a special-handling piece too big for the room left above the top goes to the
           other door and a bag comes instead. The daily ramps by stream position (12% of pieces for the first 20, up to
           36% from piece 40), identical for every player. A bag on a staging spot breaks with a double-click or a long
           press again (the old handlers tested the retired floor slots).
     PART 10, TIRE HOLLOWS (Vince: "i've STUFFED the insides of tires all the way"). A tire's hollow is (width - 1) x
       height cells (STD 6, MC 4, DB 2, FARM 10); same-size tires laid flat one on another share one hollow (two STD = 12);
       a bag is malleable and needs only its 9 cells of room; a rigid box or hazmat must fit the opening, (width - 1) wide
       and as tall as the stack; anything nested keeps the whole stack in place; the real-box nest bonus keeps its old cap.
     PART 11, BONUSES WORTH GOING FOR (Vince: "we should make these bonuses very much so worth it"). Sized against a
       measured sealable wall, about 23,000 cubes at 150 pph (design206/probe_bonus.js). A nest pays 100 + 150 per hollow
       cell (a bag in a stacked pair 340 -> 1,450); a hazmat shielded in a hollow +1,000 (was +120); STUFFED TO THE RIM
       pays 250 per hollow cell when a stack fills completely (one standard tire 1,500, a pair 3,000), only for cells no
       earlier rim paid; a roof bag 600 -> 1,000 (a wall term, so pace multiplies it); the fifth roof bag in a wall makes a
       FULL CEILING COURSE, +2,500 once. DECISION: a hazmat nests only in a stack below the waist line. It used to nest
       anywhere, and being off the grid it escaped the waist-line scan; with the shield worth 1,000 that would pay a
       hazmat for riding high. The best wall still stays far under the 400,000 arcade-sign limit (measured p90 65,000).
     Measured (design206/sim206bags.js, a greedy loader bot, 80 walls x2, ramp off vs on): bags reaching a loader once
       the wall's top passes 75% went from 0.35 to 0.40 a wall to about 1.95; every wall still sealable.
     Tests (every network call blocked): t206a 22/22, t206b 41/41, t206d 20/20, t206e 25/25, water 27/27, t205 101/101,
       tdash 20/20, t206c 73/73 at
       1366x768, 1366x657, 1366x600, 1536x864, 1920x1080, 1024x768, 768x1024, 390x844, 360x780, 844x390.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.205 - READY FOR GOODWILL: SIGN-OUT STICKS, NO HALL OF SHAME, ONE COACH VOICE, LESS DATA KEPT.
     Vince (2026-10-16): "if someone signs out they should remain signed out." / "hall of shame. probably need to
     remove anything negative before public-facing real talk." / "remove the hub voice lines too ... I'LL BRING
     THEM BACK IF I WANT TO LATER." / "we need to definitely fix the privacy wording."
       (1) ONE SIGN-OUT FOR THE WHOLE DEVICE. Signing out on any page (the game, /me/, Smalls Sort, the
           instructor dashboard) signs out all of them, in every open tab, and asks the server to end the
           session. liAuthEpoch counts sign-outs on the device; a session from before the last sign-out is
           never used, refreshed or written back. The same block lives in all four pages, and
           .tools/authblock.py refuses a build where the copies differ.
       (2) A shared computer keeps nothing of the last account: sign-out clears the remembered initials, the
           username, the recovery code, the crew code, the crew-sharing flag and the device ID (a new one is
           made next time).
       (3) SAVE MY STATS: creating an account turns it on, and the form says so. Signing in turns it on only
           where this device never recorded a choice; a recorded OFF stays off, and the sign-in banner says
           which.
       (4) Less data kept: the EMAIL box is gone and sessions send no email; the 50 local session copies,
           which nothing ever read back, are deleted.
       (5) HALL OF SHAME REMOVED from RECORDS and /me/ (slowest wall, most collapses, the grind, messiest).
           The server side that refuses those boards is FIX-09.
       (6) ONE COACH VOICE for the launch (commit 0564d17, committed alone; restore with git revert 0564d17).
           The standard voice serves every path; a saved second-voice choice is rewritten to standard.
       (7) Neutral wording, mechanics unchanged: SENT HOME is SHIFT OVER, SENT TO TRAINING is PRACTICE
           TRAILER, the hazmat and collapse lines state the safety fact instead of blaming the player, the
           repeated pick-up ladder stops guessing at motive, the New Hire level teaches instead of mocking,
           GET BACK TO WORK is KEEP LOADING.
       (8) The crew join card lists what a crew sees, says they can export it, and says they never see
           arcade initials.
       (9) The arcade account link is ON (v3.5.204 built it switched off): signed in with SAVE MY STATS on, a
           signature is linked to the account and shown only to that player on /me/. privacy.html,
           for-districts and terms describe it, as of 2026-10-16.
      (10) LICENSES AND CREW PRIVACY (Vince's answers, 2026-10-16; server side FIX-11 and FIX-11b). Crew members
           never appear on a username board; they can still sign arcade initials and win the daily, and that
           signature is not tied to their account. A free crew holds 9 members plus its leader. Free crews keep
           this in-game roster (ME); the program dashboard and CSV export come with a license. The join card and
           the owner notes say so, and the roster note no longer promises hex codes or an enterprise build.
     Tested headless with every network call mocked: the v3.5.205 suite passes 99 of 101 (the 2 were a
     report label, fixed here, and this version bump); the v3.5.204 suite's 110 behavior checks pass; the
     voice gates pass (no second-voice text left, standard pools byte-identical, 300 celebrations and 783
     comments checked).

           reads WATER n/4. Hidden in the watch-only Training demo. Fixed light colors in both themes,
           because the canvas behind it is beige in both.
       (2) THE RULES. 4 drinks a trailer. Drink 1: +150. Drink 2: +150 only if at least 3 boxes were
           loaded since drink 1 (his floor rhythm: drink, load, drink, load); bunched, it says why and
           pays nothing. Drink 3: no bonus. Drink 4: FULLY HYDRATED. After 4 the bottle is done for that
           trailer, with no penalty. The 300-cube seal bonus for 2+ drinks is retired: the same 300 is
           now paid at the drink. The 6-second cooldown stays.
       (3) WATER NO LONGER ERASES SAFETY WARNINGS. Since v3.5.59 every drink cleared the newest open
           warning of any kind (v3.5.194 kept that as an unannounced veteran trick). In a training
           product, a drink that makes a violation disappear teaches the wrong habit. A drink now
           resolves a HYDRATION warning and nothing else; a locked shelf clears the others and no longer
           clears thirst. The warning banner names the real fix for that offense.
       (4) THE 2-SECOND "COVER IT WITH WATER" WINDOW IS GONE, and so is the tumbling-box ricochet. A FLAG
           is a plain ask for a hand: a coworker takes the box you are holding and leaves a bag of
           smalls. Its cost is the cooldown and the cube penalty past three flags. The manager lines
           "HEY ," (stray comma included) and "STOP PULLING PEOPLE OFF THEIR OWN TRAILERS!" are gone.
           Next pass: FLAG becomes STAGE, the box set down on the floor where it can be picked up again.
       (5) The 4th-drink "OVER-HYDRATING ... IS SABOTAGE" penalty is gone; it contradicted his design.
       (6) BUG FIX: not drinking could become an INCIDENT although the comment said it only warns. The
           100% check called safetyWarn() a second time, and a repeated open warning escalates. A dry
           wall now gets one warning, then a reminder.
       (7) Post-sort: the "No-hydration", "thrown-away" and "Water sabotage flags" rows are removed (none
           can be non-zero now); "with the rhythm bonus" is added under Hydration breaks. The tutorial
           and the Training ladder say what the buttons now do.
       (8) PHONE FIX (same night, found by the wide-trailer study): the small bottle covered about 5 px of
           wall column 0 on a 390 px phone, so a tap there hit the bottle. It now narrows to fit the strip
           left of the wall (25 px wide at 390, 22 at 360) and lets taps through if it ever overlaps.
     Tested headless against the local file: 27 checks (desktop and phone layout, every drink, warnings,
     the 100% check, FLAG, the demo), 0 failures, no page errors.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.202 - SIGN THE WALL, AND KNOWING WHAT IS WORKING WITHOUT TRACKING ANYONE.
     Vince: "after the wall is built, there should be an option to put your 'name' on the leaderboard

       (1) SIGN THE WALL. The wall-complete card (Daily and Trailer-Load) has a three-initial box. No

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.198 - THE GAME STOPPED TEACHING THE FOLK RULE IT DOES NOT ENFORCE.
     What was wrong: eleven places told the player "fragile rides on top". The code has never
     enforced that. checkFragileCrush() crushes only weight class 5, and a fragile is safe at ANY
     height if it has a real piece on both sides (hasLateralSupport) or a locked shelf at or below
     its row (isRowLockedIn). The trailer wall is not a piece, so a fragile in a top corner is
     the exposed one. /day1/ already taught this correctly; the game contradicted its own manual.
     Worse, the trainer's commentary judged by HEIGHT (gy/GH < 0.5 = "fragile on top where it
     belongs"), so it praised the exact placement that gets crushed, and the FRAGILE_CRUSH
     write-up and the crunch banner told players to lock a shelf OVER the fragile, which protects
     nothing. What it cost: the first rule a new player reads was wrong, and every grant
     application that copied the site's "fragile on top" described a rule the game does not have.
     What changed:
       (1) Briefing rule 5 rewritten: a closed course, not height.
       (2) fireCommentary: protected vs exposed now uses the same tests the crush code uses, plus a
           row the fragile itself just completed (commentary fires before checkTetris() locks it).
       (3) Both commentary voices' fragile / frag_bad pools rewritten to match.
       (4) Crunch banner, FRAGILE_CRUSH safetyWarn line and its write-up text: "under", not "over".
       (5) Tutorial tip and checklist label for the fragile step.
       (6) Outside the game, same release: /programs/ (four lines incl. the FAQ structured data)
           and llms-full.txt (two lines). /programs/ Employer Bay card no longer says "hiring screen":
           a selection test with adverse impact must be validated under 29 CFR Part 1607, and no
           validation study exists. It now reads "a trainee practice and progress gauge".
       (7) New .tools/bump.py: moves all seven version stamps, refuses unless each is found exactly
           once, writes through a temp file. The old scratchpad copy did not survive its session.
     DECISION: the 55% "TOP ONLY" tape on fragiles (tapePreload) is untouched. It is cosmetic, no
     rule enforces it, and it is the same class v3.5.126 retired; removing art is Vince's call.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.195 - PAPER, NOT BLACK BOXES.
     Vince: "if the clipboard is there, we don't need black boxes, maybe just a paper or note.
     we can significantly downsize it if we remove the black boxes." Correct on all three counts.
     Four heavy dark panels sitting on a photographed clipboard read as UI dropped ON the art
     rather than anything written on it. The cards are notes on the page now: cream stock,
     hairline rule, the mode's colour kept as a tab down the left edge so the four stay telling
     apart. Board drops from 86vh to 72vh, because it was sized to carry the weight of those
     panels and no longer has to.
     THE MODE COLOURS WERE CHOSEN FOR A DARK CARD and wash out on paper: the green computed
     1.97:1 against the cream, nowhere near the 3:1 a non-text boundary needs. Paper gets its own
     set, every one measured: amber 6.2:1, teal 5.5:1, green 4.6:1, magenta 5.9:1. Titles go
     near-black (14.3:1) in explicit hex, not a theme token, because that page is light in BOTH
     themes and a token would invert the moment the dark toggle is on. The tab is now the only
     thing carrying mode identity, which is exactly why it had to actually be visible.
     THE SCROLLBAR THAT SCROLLED NOTHING: "the boxes also make the scroll bar that moves zero
     pixels. lol." overflow-y:auto by itself allowed a 6px left border and a drop shadow one
     pixel past the box, which produced a horizontal scrollbar across the bottom of the board
     with nowhere to go. overflow-x:hidden. Verified scrollWidth === clientWidth.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.194 - IT NEVER TOLD YOU WHY IT FELL.
     (1) THE CAUSE WAS ALWAYS BEING OVERWRITTEN. Vince: "it doesn't say why the box on top of the
     tire fell. it just falls and says whoops, slipped." True, and far wider than tires. Every
     physics sweep printed its own cause (HEAVY FREIGHT NO FULL BASE, COLUMN INTEGRITY, OVERHANG
     ON AN OVERHANG) and THEN called collapseFreeStack 220ms later, which unconditionally printed
     the generic slip line over the top of it. One or two pieces is a slip, and these sweeps drop
     exactly one or two, so the reason was buried EVERY time. The player was told "widen the base
     and it holds" for causes widening the base does not fix. The cause now travels with the
     collapse as st.why and prints once, correctly, instead of being pre-announced and buried.
     The vertical-tire path had no message at all, and the freestanding-stack rule had none in
     either branch; both have one now, and the stack rule distinguishes three-high from five-high.
     (2) BOLDER BUTTON OUTLINES. --btn-line was #8a6a2e, which computes 1.48:1 against the amber
     button face it most often sits on. The floor for a non-text boundary is 3:1, so on every
     filled button the outline was effectively not there. Light theme to #533a12 (3.13:1 on amber,
     8.46:1 on the page), dark theme to #e8b65c, and all 15 rules go 1.5px to 2px.
     (3) THE WATER WASH STOPS ADVERTISING ITSELF. Vince: "we should not announce that a drink
     clears the safety incidents. that should be a veteran easter egg with zero promotion." The
     mechanic is untouched and the record still shows the warning as washed; the sabotage ladder
     still calls out anyone farming it. What is gone is the game teaching the escape hatch the
     first time it fires. A drink reads as a drink now. You find the rest by noticing.

     Vince: "i didn't see the hazmat shelf when i made it." He was right, and it was not a near
     miss. v3.5.165 gave danger/warn lines a 1.6 second hold on the message feed so routine
     chatter could not evict a safety callout. Correct idea, but it SILENTLY DROPPED every
     non-warning message inside that window, and every award in this game is type 'ok'. The
     hazmat shelf is the worst possible victim: you earn it by handling dangerous goods, and
     handling dangerous goods is exactly what fires danger lines, so the second-largest award in
     the product (+1500) landed inside a window its own prerequisite had just opened. Earn it
     correctly, see nothing. New 'award' tier holds the feed the way danger does and can never be
     evicted by it. Given to the hazmat shelf, the seal payout, the certificate offer and both
     roof-bag lines. Amber, heavier rule, 11.6:1 on the feed ground (computed, not eyeballed).
     ALSO: 118 ESCAPED EM DASHES. The v3.5.191 sweep matched the literal U+2014 character only;

     bump script matches "// LOCK IN vX" and never this CSS-comment form. Realigned.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.192 - THE WRITE-UP GETS THE REAL BOARD, AND THE DAILY GETS A LOADOUT.
     (1) .wu-clip was a flat #f5f0e8 rectangle with a DRAWN clip, a brown rounded bar on
     ::before, imitating clipboard.png instead of using it. It is the document that STOPS the
     game and the single most important image for an instructor, and it was the last surface
     still faking the board. Real asset now, 619x905, same writing-area insets as every other
     surface. Found by searching every overlay card BY CLASS instead of by eye, after claiming
     the sweep was complete twice.
     (2) DAILY LOADOUT: 4 TIRES + 2 HAZMATS OF DIFFERENT CLASSES (Vince's spec). Four tires
     because four is enough floor to close a bottom course and a closed course is where a shelf
     comes from. TIRE-FARM joins the pool for the same reason: at 6x2 a pair covers 12 of the 17
     floor cells. It had been excluded entirely, so the daily could never roll the one tire that
     makes a floor course easy. TWO CLASSES because SEGREGATION CANNOT EXIST WITH ONE HAZMAT:
     the rule is in the code, printed in the briefing, and was structurally impossible to meet or
     break in the only mode most people will ever play. The second draw is forced to a different
     hazClass. All draws off the seeded stream in fixed order, so the daily stays identical for
     everyone. 6 of 8 slots, nothing can overflow.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.190 - THREE DEFECTS VINCE CAUGHT BY PLAYING IT.
     (1) THE CERTIFICATE WAS OFFERED EVERY WALL AND ITS PICTURE SHOWED HOLES. Not a wiring bug --
     the BAR. v3.5.186 cut CERT_MIN_PACK to 85 to make the thing reachable after two clean walls
     earned nothing, and it overshot. 85% leaves 51 of 340 cells empty, so the certificate printed
     a photograph of a wall with gaps in it under the words "A WALL LOADED CLEAN", and a loader who
     seals floor-to-ceiling cleanly earned one every single time. 95% leaves 18 empty, which reads
     as a full wall in an image. The 340/340 "PERFECT WALL" tier is untouched above it.
     certgate.js rewritten: it hardcoded the old 85 in BOTH its source lookup and its boundary
     cases, so raising the bar would have broken the gate instead of testing it. It now READS the
     constant out of this file and derives its own floor, plus a new assertion that the bar can
     never again leave more than 20 cells empty -- the picture is part of the claim.
     (2) PRINT RESULTS PRINTED THE MENU. The print stylesheet restyled #ov-postsort and never hid
     anything else, so the start menu, canvas and HUD all stayed in the print flow -- and dropping
     the report to position:static pulled it out of its overlay into normal document order, BELOW
     all of it. #ov-postsort is a direct child of <body>, so hiding every body child and bringing
     that one back is the whole fix. PRINT is what turns a score into a document an instructor
     files, so it mattered more than it looked.
     (3) A LOST NEWLINE ATE A STATEMENT. In nextTrailer(), the v3.5.185 comment ran straight into
     the code after it, so the line that hides the MAX PACK finish button on a new trailer sat
     inside the comment and never ran. Newline restored.
     No mechanic changed. Bags ride the chute exactly as they did.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.188 - THE ARROW HAD NO TAPE BEHIND IT. box.arrow was set by a bare Math.random()<0.38
     with no connection to the label, so 38% of freight drew THIS-SIDE-UP chevrons while the shipping tag on
     that same box read STANDARD or GROUND. The chevrons are not decoration: rotating one is tallied on every
     placement and docked 16 in calcScore, so the game was enforcing a rule it never printed. v3.5.126 retired
     DO NOT STACK and KEEP DRY for claiming handling the game does not model; THIS SIDE UP was swept out with
     them, and it was the one that DID have a mechanic. It rejoins the pool, and arrow now reads off the label
     instead of a coin flip. Tape and chevrons agree for the first time. Arrow pieces go from ~38% of the belt
     to ~13%, so the sideways-shipping ding appears less often and means something when it does.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.187 - THE CRUNCH BANNER LIED BY 3.75x. Crushing a box printed "-80 CUBES" and took 300.
     Two numbers for one rule: CRUNCH_PENALTY=80 was read ONLY by the banner, while calcScore carried a
     hardcoded 300. The code's own comment already said "-300 real score each", so the constant was the stale
     half and nobody noticed because the two lines are 3,100 apart. Found while mapping the mechanics for a
     recorded walkthrough -- the exact situation where reading the on-screen number aloud would have put a
     wrong figure on camera, in a product sold to instructors. One source now: the constant is 300 and
     calcScore reads it. Banner and score agree for the first time. No balance change: the real cost was
     always 300 and still is.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.186 - THE CERTIFICATE WAS UNEARNABLE. Vince played two walls on 3.5.185 and got nothing.
     Neither was a bug in the wiring: the BAR was wrong. 3.5.185 required all 340 cells. Measured against every
     wall this product has ever logged -- 203 of them -- exactly ONE reached 100% SOLIDITY, and solidity is the
     forgiving metric; pack is stricter. His two walls: floor to ceiling, integrity 100, zero collapses, 88% and
     84% packed. A bar its own author cannot clear in 203 attempts issues zero certificates, which is the same
     as not shipping one, which is the same as the credibility it was built to buy never arriving.
     THE BAR NOW READS: sealed + wall to the CEILING + 85%+ packed + integrity 100 + zero collapses on this wall
     + zero incidents on this wall. Height 100% is the literal opposite of "clicked NEXT early" -- the case he
     asked to exclude. Integrity/collapses/incidents mean nothing shifted, crushed, or fell. 85% sits above his
     own 48-wall daily average of 82.9%, so it is a good wall and not an attendance prize. Calibration on his
     two walls: the 88% daily earns it, the 84% shift with one incident does not.
     100% pack keeps its own name: the card reads THE PERFECT WALL and "every one of 340 cells filled" only
     when all 340 are filled; otherwise A WALL LOADED CLEAN and "floor to ceiling, nothing shifted, nothing
     broken". Both say CONGRATULATIONS, YOU'RE LOCKED IN. The game-achievement disclaimer is unchanged.
     SECOND DEFECT, SAME FEATURE: the earn button lived inside #np-shift, and showDailyComplete() hides that
     whole branch -- so in DAILY, the mode every new player and every visiting instructor opens first, the
     certificate could be EARNED and the button never shown. Hoisted to #next-popup, above both branches.
     The card also stopped lying: PACK was hardcoded "100%" (the old bar could only be met at 100) and
     INCIDENTS was printing the whole SESSION's incident list onto a single wall's card. Both now come off the
     graded wall. Gate proved by .tools/certgate.js -- 4 accepts, 8 refusals, run against the shipped source.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.184 - TRAINING'S SIDE PANE OUTLIVED TRAINING (Vince, live). He clicked out of the trainer

     one missing teardown: `#training-overlay` was hidden in exactly ONE place -- exitTraining() -- which is
     only reached by the explicit exit button. Leave the trainer any other way (pick a mode from the menu,
     a #daily deep link from the homepage, the browser back button) and the pane stayed up, narrating a wall
     that no longer existed, with trainingState.playing still true and its step timer still firing into a
     live game. The interactive tutorial got exactly this treatment in v3.5.137 ("clear tutorial state on any
     mode start ... so it never leaks into a real game"); the watch-and-acknowledge trainer that REPLACED it
     in v3.5.150 never inherited it. Teardown moved into hideStartMenu(), which every mode start routes
     through -- startDaily / startRegular / startShift / startGauntlet / startTutorial call it directly, and
     TOUR reaches it via openLevelSelect() -- so no mode can begin with the trainer still attached.
     Verified live: pane display:flex + timer armed -> after a mode start, display:none, playing false,
     timer cleared. THE PATTERN WORTH KEEPING: teardown belongs on the ENTRY every path shares, never on one
     exit that most paths skip.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.183 - "APPARENTLY I'M A DAY ONE" + A LIVE DATA LEAK FOUND CHASING IT.
     Vince opened his own post-sort report after a daily and it read CAREER SESSIONS 0 with every LIFETIME
     cell blank or zero -- for an account with 42 sessions, 42 walls and a 1,880 peak PPH sitting in the
     database. THE BUG: PostgREST answers with a JSON ARRAY on success and a JSON OBJECT on failure
     ({"code":"PGRST301","message":"No suitable key or wrong key type"} on an expired token, and GoTrue
     tokens live about an hour). fetchLifetime did `lifeAggregate(j||[])`, which handed that error OBJECT
     straight to the aggregator; the aggregator read `.length` off it, got undefined, skipped its loop
     entirely, and returned a FULLY ZEROED career -- which fetchLifetime then reported as state 'ok'. The
     report printed the zeros with total confidence. This is the same class as the v3.5.169/170 fix
     ("never report success you haven't read back"); that pass hardened every POST and left every GET
     exactly as it was. Now: a shared `zRows(r,j)` guard requires r.ok AND a real array, `zReadState(r)`
     separates 401 from everything else, and the four readers (fetchLifetime, fetchProfile,
     fetchFacilities, fetchCohort -- the last of which would have rendered an error as an EMPTY ROSTER on
     the paid surface) all route failures to an honest state. The report now prints "?" in every LIFETIME
     cell, " · unavailable, not zero · " in the footer, and a legend that names the cause: session expired,
     or could not reach the server, with "this wall and session are still accurate". A genuinely new
     player still reads a true 0, because a 200 with an empty array is a real answer. /me/ got the same
     hardening (v1.5): it already caught 401, while a 403 or 500 still fell through to "no walls on record".
     Verified with four stubbed responses -- 401 -> expired, 500 -> err, 200+[] -> ok/0, 200+rows -> ok/40.
     ---
     THE LEAK, found while diagnosing the above: `lockin_lifetime` was created WITHOUT security_invoker,
     so it ran as its owner and bypassed RLS on lockin_sessions completely. Measured live from a shell
     with nothing but the publishable anon key: 34 rows covering 14 distinct players' full career records
     -- sessions, scores, integrity, incident counts -- readable by anyone, with no login. me/index.html
     literally documents this view as "the RLS-scoped lockin_lifetime view (self only)". It was not.
     Migration `lifetime_view_security_invoker` sets security_invoker=on; lockin_sessions already carried
     the right policies (ls_self_select on auth.uid(), ls_instructor_select gated on consent_share AND
     facility membership), so the view now enforces exactly what every reader already assumed. Re-measured
     after: anon reads 0 rows, and 0 when targeting a known user_id directly. The public daily board still
     serves (it is public by design) and lockin_cohort -- the paid roster -- was never exposed. Audited the
     other eight tables/views from an unauthenticated shell at the same time: handles, sessions, walls,
     facilities, follows and cohort all correctly return empty.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.180 - THE TYPE FLOOR, ENFORCED. Vince has raised tiny text roughly six times over three
     months, and he is right that it keeps coming back: every previous pass fixed the INSTANCES it happened
     to look at, and nothing stopped the next one from landing. I added a clamp(8px,...) myself in v3.5.179.
     Audited the whole product: 64 sites under 11px. WHERE THEY HID: 32 in flagship CSS, 10 across the
     satellites, 4 in briefing clamp() MINIMUMS (the minimum is what ships on a short viewport, which is
     exactly the Chromebook case), and 8 in CANVAS `cx.font=` calls that no CSS grep can see -- including a
     5.5px belt index digit at 40% alpha. FIXES: the 5.5px digit is DELETED rather than enlarged, because
     nothing read it and nothing used it (no key binds to a belt slot, and the selection caret already marks
     the active piece) -- making an unreadable non-feature legible only adds clutter. In-game canvas labels
     go to 12px: the staged-piece SPEC tag (was 8px), the empty-slot SPEC/BAG placeholder (9px), the floor
     counters (10px). Belt rotation degrees and the rotation badge go to 11px. Everything else lands on the

     bigger type, and it retires the last two dead vocabularies in one edit: the "STAGE" noun (its UI was
     deleted in v3.4.11) and the "SP" acronym (retired for SPEC in v3.5.175). The briefing clamps were safe
     to raise because v3.5.179 made #brief-body scroll below 820px tall: measured at a real 1366x768, rules
     now render 13px / why-lines 11px / numbers 16px AND the whole 9-rule brief fits with 165px to spare.
     A SECOND HOLE, found by rendering rather than grepping: bare <small>/<sub>/<sup> carry NO font-size at
     all, so they inherit the browser's 0.8em shrink -- a 10px "(mobile)" label survived the size scan
     because there was no declaration to catch. 22 such tags across 9 pages now sit on an explicit
     small,sub,sup{font-size:11px} floor, declared first in each sheet (after @import, which must stay
     first) so every class-specific rule still outranks it.
     **GATE INSTALLED: `.tools/typefloor.py`.** It fails the build on any font-size, clamp() minimum, or
     canvas font under 11px, AND on any page carrying bare shrink tags without a floor rule. Verified by
     stripping a floor and watching it fire. This is the part that makes it stop coming back -- the rule
     existed already; enforcement did not. Raising the floor product-wide is now one constant in that file.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.173 - THE DEVS SLOP-CULL (21-item ledger, Desktop\DEVS-LEDGER.txt). Their verdict: the slop was
     REAL BUT SHALLOW · four root causes, not dozens. (1) EVERY cross-screen contradiction the owner saw was ONE bug:
     fetchLifetime queried lockin_lifetime with `&mode=eq.gameMode` and read a single row, while SOCIAL/ME queried
     unfiltered and SUMMED every mode · yet both were labelled LIFETIME/CAREER. The deltas WERE the daily row
     (12 vs 15 incidents, 29 vs 36 sessions, 632 vs 783 shelves, 592 vs 734 double-locks, 77 vs 143 nests). Now one
     shared lifeAggregate(rows) folds the per-mode rows for BOTH surfaces · counts sum, peak takes a clamped max,
     averages are session-weighted · so they are mathematically incapable of disagreeing again. (2) PEAK PPH =
     3,600,000: getPPH() guarded only el<=0, not a sub-millisecond window, so the first piece after a clock start read
     1 box / 0.001s x 3600 = 3.6M, Math.max latched it forever, and it shipped to the server as a career record.
     Added PPH_FLOOR_S=5 + PPH_CAP=2000 inside getPPH itself, so every consumer (HUD, score multiplier, report,
     stored lifetime) is protected at the source; avgPPH got the same treatment; calcScore stopped carrying its own
     second inline pph formula. Latch, write and read are all clamped, and 21 poisoned server rows were scrubbed to
     "not measured" rather than faked to the cap · the honest peak is now 1,880 (his real best), not a number nobody
     earned. (3) THE CLIPBOARD (asked for a month): every board was pinned to width:min(4xx,9xvw) · ~15% of a desktop
     screen, too small for four leaderboard rows. The art is portrait 619x905, so the binding constraint is HEIGHT:
     all four surfaces now size by viewport height (up to 1120px) with width following the ratio · undistorted and
     ~1.6x bigger. (4) THE FULL REPORT joined the clipboard family: it was the ONE surface still on a flat tan sheet.
     Stretching the PNG under a wide data report would have fattened its centred metal clip, so the report is now
     built from the SAME materials · the board’s grey shell, its cream paper inset, and a LOCK-IN clip masthead ·
     consistent without warping the art and still wide enough to read three numeric columns. Plus the outline law on
     the report + social buttons (PRINT/CLOSE, .pf-tab, FOLLOW, .lb-tab → 1.5px var(--btn-line), 6px, 34px).

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.170 - ROUND-TRIP PROOF + REPRESENTATION. The v3.5.169 post-mortem exposed the real design sin:
     the client BELIEVED every post it never checked. Now the daily submit is verified against reality · after the
     server accepts a score the game re-reads the PUBLIC board and physically looks for the player’s name. Confirmed
     shows "✓ CONFIRMED ON THE WORLD BOARD"; a readable board that does NOT contain them shows "NOT ON THE BOARD"
     with what to do; an unreadable board reports unknown and is never treated as success. Silence can no longer pass
     for a posted score. REPRESENTATION (why 19 signed-up players saw no trace of themselves): the DAILY board only
     ever holds people who played THAT date, and the boards that hold EVERY player who ever sealed a wall ·
     lockin_total_board / lockin_alltime_board · were buried in the 4th tab, capped at the top 5. Nine real ranked
     players were sitting in the database unseen. HALL cards now pull 10, and TOTAL LOADED leads the fame column
     because it is the one board every loader appears on.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.169 - **CRITICAL: DEAD SESSIONS WERE SILENTLY EATING PLAYERS’ SCORES.** Found while debugging a
     live report during a Facebook push. isSignedIn() only checked that a uid EXISTED, never whether the token was
     still valid · so a session that expired days earlier still read as signed in: the corner showed your name, every
     POST carried the dead JWT, Supabase answered 401/403, and the client threw the response away. Proven live on the
     owner’s own browser: stored token 10.7 DAYS past expiry, server replying 403 invalid JWT, isSignedIn() returning
     true. The satellites got this exact fix in v3.5.160; THE GAME NEVER DID. Now: zSessionAlive() (uid + token +
     inside expiry) backs isSignedIn(), the corner button and the ACCOUNT status; zWithAuth() refreshes before every
     post and, when the refresh token is also dead, calls zSessionDead() · which clears storage, flips the UI to
     CLOCK-IN and says so in a banner; a 401/403 on any write is treated as an expiry; the daily result panel now
     reports "NOT POSTED · YOUR SESSION EXPIRED" with what to do about it instead of inventing a rank. Boot no longer
     trusts a stored session.
     ALSO: **HOMEPAGE LIVE COUNTER** (asked for weeks ago, finally shipped) · new SECURITY DEFINER RPC lockin_stats()
     returns aggregate counts ONLY (no rows, no identities, RLS untouched); the strip now shows WALLS LOADED and
     LOADERS SIGNED ON, live, degrading silently to em-dashes if the backend is unreachable.
     ALSO: **GA4 REMOVED ENTIRELY** (owner decision, recorded): loader, config, CSP origins, and the privacy §07 claim.
     District reviewers scan for googletagmanager.com exactly like ad tech, the product already owns consent-gated
     first-party telemetry, and traffic today is small enough that the measurement loss is negligible · so "we ship
     zero third-party analytics" is now literally true. gaEvent/gaPlay remain as inert no-ops. Do not re-add.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.167 - RE-VERDICT VERIFICATION PASS (adversarial self-audit of every blocker fix). Three real
     findings, all fixed. (1) DAILY DETERMINISM, ROUND TWO: triggerCollapse chose how many boxes to shed with
     rnd(1,2) · Math.random · and collapses happen in the daily. Two players making byte-identical moves could lose a
     different number of boxes and a different -80/box. The v3.5.166 audit only covered the GENERATION stream; this was
     CONSEQUENCE randomness. In daily the shed count now derives from the date seed + this wall’s collapse index

     "added then overwritten in the same placeBox call" pattern the Gatekeeper cited, since calcScore’s shelfB term
     ((G.tetrisTotal)*TETRIS_BONUS) already pays every lock. Removed: one scoring source now, no behavior change.

   CHANGE LOG v2.4.6 · LOOP WATCHDOG (silent-stall fix + diagnosis). The mid-wall freeze does NOT throw (window.__LI_ERR was never set) · the render loop silently stops advancing. New watchdog: the loop ticks a heartbeat every frame; a 1.2s timer checks it. If the heartbeat stalls while the game should be running (not paused, not game-over, no menu/pause/end/report screen up), the loop is AUTO-RESTARTED so you can never be stuck again, and a one-time amber strip prints the exact state (paused/gOver/mode/placed/wallH/animF/lastLoopT) to window.__LI_STALL + console. It also catches a degenerate canvas (W or H collapsed) and re-runs resize(). This both heals the freeze live AND tells us the cause on the next occurrence. No gameplay change.

   CHANGE LOG v3.5.134 - AFK FALSE-KICK FIXED (a playtester: "says 5, kicked out at 2:30" on a 2-min session). The AFK idle timer (idleSec) and the low-rate timer (pphLowSec) never reset per session · a prior game left idleSec high, and a fresh game that happened to start with placed===_afkLastPlaced===0 kept accumulating the OLD idle, so the 5-minute AFK kick fired minutes early. FIX: track the session clock (_afkStartT); startT is set fresh on every wall/game start (and advanced on pause-resume), so any startT change zeroes the idle + low-rate clocks. Also made restartGame reset placed + startT (it was carrying the prior run's clock · a fresh run now starts at 0 elapsed). Verified: a new session with a carried-over idleSec=250 resets to 0 (no false kick), the AFK still fires at the REAL 300s, and a placement resets idle. NEXT: the floating-small physics (bottom small breaks, top stays floating).
   CHANGE LOG v3.5.133 - CRITICAL: DAILY FREEZE FIXED (the v3.5.123 regression that hit 2000 players). Finishing a daily wall FROZE the game · gOver went true but NO end screen showed, so controls were dead and admin was unreachable ("everything glitched, no admin after daily"). ROOT CAUSE: daily called endGame(reason, TRUE); endGame's win-path does `if(win){sealWall(reason);return;}`, which re-entered sealWall's daily branch, which re-called endGame · but gOver was already set, so `if(gOver)return` blocked it and the ov-end screen never rendered. FIX: every daily end path now routes through endGame(reason, FALSE) · the non-win path that OWNS gOver, shows the DAILY DONE screen, and runs dailyFinish() (submit + board). The re-seal path and the FINISH/nextTrailer path are guarded to end daily too (no stray NEXT TRAILER popup · that button was the "glitch"). Verified: full wall + FINISH click both show DAILY DONE + submit + gOver, and TRY AGAIN replays the same seed. No freeze, no dead controls.
   CHANGE LOG v3.5.132 - MEASUREMENT WHOLE + TOTAL (the raw per-action timeline). zLog captured ONLY placements · the max-resolution layer under the dashboard was blind to everything else. Now every action is logged to the per-session event stream, timestamped (deciseconds from sessionStartT): place, reject, rotate, lift, flag(throw), water, bagbreak, seal (with density/fill/pph/pieces), slip, collapse, safety warn, safety incident, writeup. The stream RESETS per session (was accumulating across sessions in the same tab · timestamps are relative to sessionStartT, so it must zero at the session boundary; reset happens right before the first place is logged so nothing is dropped). Combined with the v3.5.128 unified density and the v3.5.129 telemetry-accuracy pass, the dashboard can now reconstruct exactly how any wall was built AND every number (grade / seal payout / per-wall board / session summary) is one consistent figure. Verified: a mixed action burst logs rotate/water/warn/incident/flag/writeup/reject/slip/collapse/seal, each as [decisec, kind, data]. Measurement is complete.
